| TOP-W | **Restricted target (gap).** Rename this part's leading upper-bound coefficient `C_TOP(L)`. For the foregoing family only, at every top level n, for the specified class minimizer U of V and compatible W, Y_n(W) := E_0(U;W_0)-E_n(V;W_n) <= C_TOP(L) I_n^grad(V;W_n^dagger) + C_cub(L) sum_(i<=n) L^(-(n-i)/2) cub(V_i;W_i^dagger). (TOP-slow) The enlargements must have a bounded ratio to W at the weights/rates read by the consumer. Constants are to be free of n, volume, x and location. Only the upper sign is needed by hole-filling; EX-loc supplies the separate lower control with cubic errors. Together with cubic decay and energy comparability, this target suffices for the scalar FALL/COMP arguments on levels 1 through k. DS additionally needs SRC/CRUDE, domain and tail control, and a strictly positive margin between its response rates and norm rates. The fine endpoint is separate. Thus restriction to this weight family removes an unnecessary false requirement; it does not close TOP-W or DS. | `library/excess-consumer-weight-continuation.md:45`; `library/excess-localized-competitor-stop.md:359` | not in their account | none | open | — | — |
| TP1-AG | **AG — AG-k1*.** For the same positive stopped law and smooth-test construction, uniformly in \(j,c\), \[ \sum_{b\ge0}L^{8b}\mathbb P^{(j)}(\mathfrak L_j^{\ge b}(c)) \le C_{\rm AG}e^{-c_{\rm AG}(\log x_j)^2}. \tag{2.11} \] We use it only at \(j=k_1\). This is the \(\check R\)-form of [ag1-draft2.md](ag1-draft2.md) (8.1) combined with the running-minimum step \(\min_i x_i\ge x_j-2c_2\) of this text (§4); ag1-draft2's own passage from \(\check R_k\ge(\log x_k)^r\) to the \((\log x)^2\) rate omits that step and must be repaired there. It is the output of ag1-draft2 §8, with the trigger input supplied conditionally by [ag1-tt-draft.md](ag1-tt-draft.md). Its named upstream inputs are (K)+(MI), (COV), (SM), (RS), (BGL), (R0)–(R3), (LT), LG1(3), and, in place of (TT), the conditional-law package (CL) with its strengthened room (5.4′). **Outstanding:** (CL)(P) requires derivative and Hessian bounds on the non-Gaussian conditional exponent throughout the small-field region. Its proposed analytic supplier (P-an) is drafted in [pan-draft.md](pan-draft.md), which recasts (P-an)(d) as a uniform Kotecky-Preiss statement, proves (P-an) as an implication, and names (L) ([nl1-L-draft.md](nl1-L-draft.md)) as its first input. None of this is refereed. Other upstream imported estimates, including (LT), also remain imports. Neither (2.11) nor those estimates are asserted here unconditionally. The running-minimum convention is retained; if the upstream bound is first stated as \(e^{-c\check R_j}\), Lemma 4.1 yields (2.11) after reducing \(c_{\rm AG}\). | `library/tp1.md:148` | not in their account | `TP1` | OPEN | t0-tree AG | [ J3 ](t0-tree.md#node-j3) |
| TP1-AMP | **AMP — TP-live, TP-far and the first-derivative oscillation.** There are \(y\)-independent numbers \(c_i\) and a fixed \(Q\) such that, with derivatives at zero, \[ \begin{split} &#124;\partial_i(\Phi^{\rm pt}+\Phi^{\rm reg})-c_i&#124; &\le Cx^{-1}(\log x)^Qt_i(DL^{-n})^2 &&\text{on }\Lambda^c,\\ &#124;\partial_i(\Phi^{\rm pt}+\Phi^{\rm reg})-c_i&#124; &\le Cx^{-1}(\log x)^Qt_i &&\text{on all histories},\\ &#124;\partial_i\Phi^{\rm live}&#124;&\le Cx^{-1}(\log x)^Qt_i L^{4A}1_\Lambda,\\ &#124;\partial_{12}\Phi^{\rm live}&#124;&\le Cx^{-2}(\log x)^Qt_ft_h L^{8A}1_\Lambda,\\ &#124;\partial_i\Phi^{\rm far}&#124;&\le Cx^{-1}e^{-cD}t_i,\qquad &#124;\partial_{12}\Phi^{\rm far}&#124;\le Cx^{-2}e^{-cD}t_ft_h . \end{split} \tag{2.10} \] The live part vanishes off \(\Lambda\), and the failure of the required top-level small-field condition is included in \(\Lambda\). The contact neighbourhood meets at most \(C(M\check R_{k_1})^4\) cells. **Outstanding:** these bounds, particularly the factors \(x^{-1},x^{-2}\) and only logarithmic prefactors, are not supplied by SA's displayed age-amplitude bound. That bound also has factors exponential in \(\check R\). We do not claim the weaker AG rate absorbs those factors for arbitrary \(r\). | `library/tp1.md:132` | not in their account | `TP1` | OPEN | t0-tree AMP | [ J3 ](t0-tree.md#node-j3) |
| TP1-FLOW | **FLOW — IN1, OL1 and the constant-source part of SC1.** The admitted flows exist and are independent of unused finer cutoff and torus size. They satisfy \(b_j\le B_\sharp\) and \[ b_j=A_j^0-A_{j-1}^0+e_j,\qquad &#124;A_j^0-jc_0&#124;\le c_2,\qquad &#124;e_j&#124;\le Cx_{j-1}^{-1/2}(\log x_{j-1})^{\mathfrak p} +Cx_{j-1}^{-\alpha},\quad \alpha=(\kappa_0-4)/2>1. \tag{2.1} \] The source changes \(\beta\) to \(\beta-t\), with all auxiliary choices frozen. The resulting increments \(b_j(t)\) are holomorphic for \(&#124;t&#124;<2\varrho\), real on real \(t\), and \[ s_j=b_j'(0),\quad &#124;s_j&#124;\le C\{x_{j-1}^{-3/2}(\log x_{j-1})^{\mathfrak p_0} +\varrho^{-1}x_{j-1}^{-\alpha}\},\qquad \zeta_k=1+\sum_{j=1}^k s_j=-\left.\frac{d x_k(t)}{dt}\right&#124;_{0}. \tag{2.2} \] Write \(\zeta=\zeta_{k_0}\). These are telescoped one-loop bounds; no convergence of individual one-loop increments is assumed. | `library/tp1.md:54` | not in their account | `TP1` | OPEN | t0-tree FLOW | [ J3 ](t0-tree.md#node-j3) |
| TP1-FREE | **FREE — FC1 and the free decomposition with a terminal bound.** On each compatible torus the scalar plaquette covariance \(P\) of the standard Gaussian on \(\operatorname{ran}d_1\) has positive semidefinite slice covariances \(G_j\) and residual background covariances \(R_j\), with \[ P=\sum_{i=1}^jG_i+R_j,\qquad &#124;G_j(p,q)&#124;\le C(a/a_j)^4e^{-\delta &#124;x_p-x_q&#124;/a_j},\qquad &#124;R_j(p,q)&#124;\le C(a/a_j)^4. \tag{2.3} \] A fixed one-level change in the slice convention is absorbed in \(C,\delta\). For orientations \(P_0,Q_0\), and \(a\le &#124;u&#124;/10\le\ell/100\), \[ &#124;a^{-4}P(p_{P_0},q_{Q_0})-\mathbb K_{P_0Q_0}(u)&#124; \le C&#124;u&#124;^{-4}\{(a/&#124;u&#124;)^{1/2}+(&#124;u&#124;/\ell)^4\},\quad u=x_p-x_q, \] \[ \mathbb K(u)=\pi^{-2}&#124;u&#124;^{-4}\Lambda^2(I-2\hat u\hat u^{\,T}), \qquad \sum_{P_0,Q_0}\mathbb K_{P_0Q_0}(u)^2=6\pi^{-4}&#124;u&#124;^{-8}. \tag{2.4} \] **Outstanding:** the full volume-uniform package (2.3)–(2.4) is imported, not proved here. In particular, the bound on \(R_j\) is additional to a bound on the slices. The source decomposition does not say \(R_K=0\). | `library/tp1.md:71` | not in their account | `TP1` | OPEN | t0-tree FREE | [ J3 ](t0-tree.md#node-j3) |
| TP1-LIMIT | **LIMIT — CL1 and PH1.** Along the specified volume-first limits, centred first and second moments converge on separated test tuples. Every component under consideration has its own all-state realization, including convergence of its centred first and second moments (used in Lemma 7.1), with OS vectors built from centred fields, by periodic Gibbs states at admitted data with \(K\to\infty\); a mixture identity alone does not suffice. The limits and realized components are normalized real functionals with the reflection-positive OS construction. Write \(\vartheta(u_1,u_2,u_3,u_4)=(u_1,u_2,u_3,-u_4)\) and \((\vartheta f)(u)=f(\vartheta u)\). The vacuum \(\Omega=[1]\) has norm one, \(\langle\Omega,[O(f)]\rangle=\sigma(O(f))\), and for real positive-time \(f\), the vector \(v_f=[O(f)]-\sigma(O(f))\Omega\) satisfies \(\|v_f\|^2=S_2^T[\sigma](f,\vartheta f)\). In each realized component the nonnegative self-adjoint Hamiltonian \(H\) satisfies \(\ker H=\mathbb C\Omega\); uniqueness of the vacuum is not required of a mixture. Conclusions for components have exactly the qualification of this realization input, for example almost every component if that is what PH1 supplies. | `library/tp1.md:165` | not in their account | `TP1` | OPEN | t0-tree LIMIT | [ J3 ](t0-tree.md#node-j3) |
| TP1-PRES | **PRES — SC1's exact formed-source identity.** At level \(k_1\) there is a positive unsourced law \(\mathbb P\) on retained histories, block fields and stored variables \(y\), and a holomorphic exponent \[ Z(z)/Z(0)=\mathbb E_{\mathbb P}e^{\Phi(z;y)},\quad \Phi(0;y)=0,\quad \Phi=\Phi^{\rm pt}+\Phi^{\rm reg}+\Phi^{\rm live}+\Phi^{\rm far}, \tag{2.7} \] with differentiation through the expectation valid twice near zero. Here \(Z\) is the Wilson partition function with source \(z_1f+z_2h\); \(\Phi^{\rm pt}=\sum_p\varphi_p(z_1f(x_p)+z_2h(x_p);y)\). The marginal on histories is exactly the law used in AG. **Outstanding:** [ref-73.md](ref-73.md) establishes an abstract transport identity; identifying its logarithm with this formed exponent, with the following bounds, remains an input. | `library/tp1.md:103` | not in their account | `TP1` | OPEN | t0-tree PRES | [ J3 ](t0-tree.md#node-j3) |
| TP1-REG | **REG — strengthened TP-cx and the Gaussian identification.** There is a deterministic common family \(\mathcal D_j\) of localisation domains, with \(\Phi^{\rm reg}=\sum_{j\le k_1}\sum_{X\in\mathcal D_j} e_j(X;z,y)\). Each domain has tree size \(m_j(X)\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}\), a dependence region \(X^+\) of diameter at most \(CMa_j(m_j(X)+1)\), and the number of size-\(m\) domains whose dependence region meets a given level-\(j\) localisation cube is at most \(Ce^{\epsilon m}\). Here \(\kappa>\epsilon\). The energies are independent of a source whose support misses \(X^+\), and holomorphic on \[ &#124;z_i&#124;t_i<r_{\rm cx}x_j\quad\hbox{for each source meeting }X^+, \qquad (t_1,t_2)=(t_f,t_h). \tag{2.8} \] All histories satisfy \(&#124;e_j&#124;\le Ce^{-\kappa m_j(X)}\); each \(e_j(X;\cdot)\) is independent of any source whose support misses \(X^+\). There are deterministic Gaussian energies \(e_j^G(X;z)\) on the same family, bounded by the same estimate, independent of any source whose support misses \(X^+\), and holomorphic on the discs (2.8), such that, on those discs, for every \(y\notin\Lambda\) and every \(j,X\), \[ &#124;e_j(X;z,y)-e_j^G(X;z)&#124; \le Cx_j^{-1/2}(\log x_j)^q e^{-\kappa m_j(X)},\qquad \sum_{j,X}\partial_{12}e_j^G(X;0)=\Gamma(f,h). \tag{2.9} \] The sums are normally convergent on smaller discs. **Outstanding:** the draft's TP-cx only compares a domain when its background is regular. Absence of a live component near the supports at \(k_1\) does not by itself make all earlier domains regular, or complete a history-dependent domain list to one deterministic Gaussian list. The all-level comparison and common completion in (2.9) must be supplied, including previously healed regions. Its last equality, called **G-ID**, is also an import: the source-transport formula alone does not prove that these energies have exactly the Gaussian model (2.6). The supremum-norm discs (2.8), including levels \(j>k_0\), are stronger than the ordinary source ball. | `library/tp1.md:115` | not in their account | `TP1` | OPEN | t0-tree REG | [ J3 ](t0-tree.md#node-j3) |
| TP1-STATE | **STATE — LG1 and SR1.** Compatible torus measures have subsequential local limits that are periodic Gibbs states. Every state in this class is invariant under lattice translations. There are a fixed cell containing a unit ball with positive boundary buffer, constants \(Q_0\ge1,r_0>0\), and a source norm satisfying \(\|F\|_\sharp\le C(\|F\|_\infty+\operatorname{Lip}F)\), such that for any two real tests in that ball there is one state-independent holomorphic \(E_C(z)\) for which \[ h_\omega(z)=e^{-E_C(z)} \omega(e^{z_1O(F_1)+z_2O(F_2)}),\qquad h_\omega(0)=1,\quad &#124;h_\omega&#124;\le Q_0 \] on \(\sum_i&#124;z_i&#124;\|F_i\|_\sharp<r_0\), for every periodic Gibbs state \(\omega\) at the datum. This includes signed tests, finite sums of translates and overlapping supports. The common normalizer, not an estimate separately normalized for each state, is essential. | `library/tp1.md:156` | not in their account | `TP1` | OPEN | t0-tree STATE | [ J3 ](t0-tree.md#node-j3) |
